It serves recruiters/hiring managers and the design-engineering community equally: personality on the surface, key information (experience, projects, contact) reachable in seconds.\n8\t\n9\t**Core Value:** The site must feel crafted and memorable (tactile, physics-based motion and strong visual identity) while key info stays instantly accessible, on mobile as much as desktop.\n10\t\n11\t### Constraints\n12\t\n13\t- **Tech stack**: Vite + React + TypeScript + Tailwind — required by motion-primitives and react-spring\n14\t- **Hosting**: GitHub Pages — static output only, needs Actions workflow or a built-output branch\n15\t- **Platform**: Mobile must be excellent — touch interactions, performance on phones\n16\t- **Accessibility/perf**: Assumed baseline: `prefers-reduced-motion` respected, good Lighthouse scores (to confirm with user)\n17\t- **Process**: Detailed documentation before coding; research and surface findings for tooling decisions\n18\t\n19\t<!-- GSD:project-end -->\n20\t\n21\t<!-- GSD:stack-start source:research/STACK.md -->\n22\t\n23\t## Technology Stack\n24\t\n25\t## Bottom Line\n26\t\n27\t## Recommended Stack\n28\t\n29\t### Core Framework\n30\t\n31\t| Technology | Version (verified 2026-10-08) | Purpose | Why | Confidence |\n32\t|------------|-------------------------------|---------|-----|------------|\n33\t| Vite | `^8.3.4` | Build/dev server | Current `latest`. `create-vite` 9.2.1 scaffolds it. That is acceptable for a portfolio, but it is the marginal price of the \"both libraries\" decision, so use react-spring where it earns it.\n91\t- Full `motion` component is the heavy part (about 53 kB with presence/spring/gesture features). motion.dev's own figures: `motion` about 34 kB, `m` + `LazyMotion` about 4.6 kB initial (+15 kB `domAnimation`, +25 kB `domMax`), `useAnimate` mini about 2.3 kB. **Optional optimization (not tested):** because motion-primitives is copy-pasted source, you could swap `motion.x` for `m.x` and wrap the app in `LazyMotion`. `AnimatePresence`/`layout`/`drag` need `domMax`, which erodes the saving. Do this only if Lighthouse demands it.\n92\t- Code-split below-the-fold sections with `React.lazy` so the hero ships alone. Any 3D must be lazy-loaded (see Gaps).\n93\t- Not measured: runtime frame rate on a real phone. That belongs in an early performance spike.\n94\t\n95\t## The Four Named Tools\n96\t\n97\t### 1. motion-primitives (ibelick) : USE, as a copy-in source library. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH\n98\t\n99\t- Only deps are `motion`, `clsx`, `tailwind-merge`, `lucide-react`, plus a `@/lib/utils` `cn()` helper. **No shadcn init required.**\n100\t- Components are served as shadcn-registry JSON at `https://motion-primitives.com/c/<name>.json` (HTTP 200 verified).\n101\t- The official CLI is `npx motion-primitives@latest add <name>`. It works (tested) but the npm package is stale (0.1.0, March 2025) and **drops files into `./components/motion-primitives/` at the project root, not `src/`**. Either move them to `src/components/motion-primitives/` or skip the CLI and copy the registry JSON content. Decide once and document it.\n102\t- Needs the `@` alias in both `vite.config.ts` (`resolve.alias`) and `tsconfig.app.json` (`paths: { \"@/*\": [\"./src/*\"] }`). With TS 6/7, `paths` works **without** `baseUrl` (verified in the probe). shadcn's Vite doc still says to add `baseUrl`. You do not need it.\n103\t\n104\t### 2. react-spring : USE, scoped. Confidence: HIGH\n105\t\n106\t- Install `@react-spring/web@~10.1.2`. Import `useSpring, useSprings, useTrail, useChain, useTransition, animated, config` from `@react-spring/web`.\n107\t- Version note: `react-spring` (the umbrella, 10.0.4) is behind and v11.0.0-beta (beta.1 released 2026-10-01) is **removing it**, going ESM-only, and deprecating `useTransition` in favor of new `usePresence`/`usePresenceList`. Stay on 10.1.x stable. Avoid `useTransition` for new core UI (use motion's `AnimatePresence`) so the v11 migration stays trivial.\n108\t- Reduced motion is built in: `useReducedMotion` is exported from `@react-spring/web` (confirmed in the type definitions), and the global flag `Globals.assign({ skipAnimation: true })` snaps every spring to its target. Wire it once (snippet below).\n109\t- Dependencies are tiny and tree-shakable. Measured at about +17 kB gz for `useSpring/useTrail/useSprings`.\n110\t\n111\t### 3. realtimecolors.com : USE as a design tool, with caveats. Confidence: HIGH\n112\t\n113\t- The seed (`050315 / fbfbfe / 2f27ce / dedcff / 433bff`, Inter/Inter) is Text, Background, Primary, Secondary, Accent. Note `050315` on `fbfbfe` is the **light** theme pairing (dark text on light bg). The tool's light/dark toggle (Alt+T) lets you design both modes in one session. The project requires light and dark, so design both explicitly rather than inverting.\n114\t- It shows AA/AAA contrast traffic lights per role. Use them as the accessibility gate for text and interactive colors.\n115\t- Exports: CSS variables, Tailwind CSS, SCSS, and custom templates, in HEX/RGB/HSL/OKLAB/OKLCH. The tool lists a `tailwind.config.js` style Tailwind export and does not mention v4. **Export \"CSS\" with OKLCH and paste into `@theme`**, not the Tailwind-config export:\n116\t- Its type-scale presets (1.067 to 1.618) map to a `--text-*` ladder in `@theme`.\n117\t- The user wants alternatives explored: do that in the design phase (Realtime Colors' scheme generators: analogous/complementary/triadic, plus randomize with Space). Keep exactly 5 role tokens plus derived tints so swapping palettes later is a single-block edit.\n118\t\n119\t### 4. godly.design/posts : USE as inspiration only, not a dependency. Confidence: MEDIUM\n120\t\n121\t- `godly.design/posts` is a curated **feed of linked social posts** (mostly X links to designers' UI interaction clips), with category nav (Websites, Tools, Templates, etc.). No dates, tags, RSS, or API were visible. Treat it as a mood-board source: capture screenshots/links into the planning docs, then extract motion patterns (spring feel, press states, cursor effects) into a written reference list before coding. (Probe also noted `godly.website` currently 301-redirects to `recent.design`, so the brand's structure may have shifted. Rely on `godly.design/posts/` and verify when you actually browse.)\n122\t- No code or package implications.\n123\t\n124\t## Division of Responsibility (motion vs react-spring)\n125\t\n126\t| Concern | Owner | Why |\n127\t|---------|-------|-----|\n128\t| Mount/unmount, `AnimatePresence`, exit animations | **motion** | Mature, handles React 19 strict mode and transitions (13.1.1 and 13.4.6 fixes). |\n129\t| Layout and shared-element transitions (`layout`, `layoutId`, Morphing Dialog) | **motion** | react-spring has no equivalent. |\n130\t| Scroll-linked values (`useScroll`, `useTransform`, ScrollProgress, in-view reveals) | **motion** | Already bundled. 13.4.x moved offsets onto hardware-accelerated `ViewTimeline` where supported. |\n131\t| Text/number effects, stagger reveals, all motion-primitives prefabs | **motion** | They are written against it. |\n132\t| Simple drag, `whileTap`, `whileHover` | **motion** | Built in. No extra gesture lib. |\n133\t| Velocity-carrying physics: flick/toss, drag-release with momentum, rubber-band, pointer-chasing follower, squish-on-press toys | **react-spring** | `useSpring` with continuous retargeting is its sweet spot and gives the \"tactile\" signature. |\n134\t| Staggered chains of springs (`useTrail`, `useChain`) for playful sets of elements | **react-spring** | Concise API. |\n135\t| Anything in `@react-spring/three` | **react-spring** | Only if 3D is adopted. |\n136\t\n137\t## Tailwind v4 Notes\n138\t\n139\t- v4 `hover:` only applies under `@media (hover: hover)`, so hover styles never stick after a tap on phones. This matches the \"no desktop-only gestures\" requirement. Do **not** restore v3 behavior with `@custom-variant hover (&:hover)`.\n140\t- Browser floor: Safari 16.4+, Chrome 111+, Firefox 128+ (uses `@property`, `color-mix()`). Fine for this audience.\n141\t- No `tailwind.config.js`; `tw-animate-css` (the shadcn replacement for tailwindcss-animate) is not needed since motion handles animation.\n142\t\n143\t## Dark Mode (covered: Tailwind v4 + tiny script)\n144\t\n145\t## Font Loading\n146\t\n147\t- `import '@fontsource-variable/inter/wght.css'` in `main.tsx`. Verified: it declares `font-display: swap`, `font-weight: 100 900`, and per-script `unicode-range`, so a browser downloads only the **latin subset (48 kB woff2)** for English copy (latin-ext is 85 kB and only fetched if used). Vite emits all subsets to `dist/` (harmless unused files).\n148\t- `@fontsource-variable/inter/opsz.css` adds optical sizing (latin 73 kB). Worth it only if you want display-size tuning.\n149\t- **Preload is not automatic**: a bare `<link rel=\"preload\" href=\"@fontsource-variable/inter/files/...\">` in `index.html` is *not* rewritten by Vite (verified, stays unresolved). If you want a preload, use a small `transformIndexHtml` plugin or `import url from '...woff2?url'`. Skipping preload is acceptable with `font-display: swap`; revisit if Lighthouse flags it.\n150\t- If the design adds a display face from Realtime Colors, use the same Fontsource route (one variable family, latin only), and keep to 2 families total for mobile performance.\n151\t\n152\t## GitHub Pages Deploy\n153\t\n154\t## Alternatives Considered\n155\t\n156\t| Category | Recommended | Alternative | Why Not |\n157\t|----------|-------------|-------------|---------|\n158\t| Framework | Vite + React | Next.js / Astro | Next explicitly out of scope. Astro would be lighter on JS but the user wants React islands everywhere and these two React animation libs. |\n159\t| Animation split | motion for UI choreography + react-spring for signature physics | react-spring only | Loses `layoutId`/`AnimatePresence`/scroll and every motion-primitives component. |\n160\t| Animation split | (same) | motion only | Viable and smaller (saves 17 kB), but the user explicitly wants to try react-spring. Revisit if the spike shows react-spring adds nothing. |\n161\t| react-spring package | `@react-spring/web` | `react-spring` umbrella | Lagging, `deprecated` tag present, removed in v11 beta. |\n162\t| Installing primitives | Copy via registry JSON / CLI, then patch | Wait for an npm package | None exists. Source ownership is the model. |\n163\t| Styling | Tailwind v4 | Tailwind v3.4 | Only for pre-2023 browsers. motion-primitives and shadcn are on v4. |\n164\t| TypeScript | 6.0.x | 7.0.x | 7.0.2 compiled the probe cleanly, so it is available, but v6 is the scaffold default. Take 7 post-launch. |\n165\t| Dark mode | Tailwind data-attr + inline script | next-themes | Next-oriented abstraction we do not need. |\n166\t| Fonts | Fontsource self-hosted | Google Fonts `<link>` | Third-party request, render-blocking, privacy. |\n167\t\n168\t## What NOT to Use\n169\t\n170\t| Do not use | Why |\n171\t|------------|-----|\n172\t| `react-spring` umbrella package | Deprecated tag, removed in v11. Use `@react-spring/web`. |\n173\t| `react-spring` v11 beta | Beta, breaking `onRest`/`immediate` semantics. Wait for stable. |\n174\t| Raw `framer-motion` imports | motion 14 depends on `framer-motion` internally. Always import from `motion/react` so there is one copy. |\n175\t| motion-primitives `Cursor`, `Magnetic`, `Tilt`, `Spotlight` as *primary* interactions | Mouse-only. They are desktop garnish. |\n176\t| `shadcn init` | Unneeded; adds Radix/CVA you will not use. |\n177\t| `tailwind.config.js`, `@tailwind base/components/utilities` | v3 patterns. |\n178\t| `@use-gesture/react` by default | Last published March 2024, and motion already provides drag/tap/hover. Only add if a react-spring-driven drag cannot be done well otherwise. |\n179\t| `vite-plugin-image-optimizer` as the primary image tool | Compress-only (no srcset/format variants). Last publish Oct 2025. |\n180\t| Hard-coded absolute asset paths | Break when `base` changes. |\n181\t| Both libraries animating the same element/property | The one real coexistence hazard. |\n182\t\n183\t## Gaps: capabilities the four tools do NOT cover\n184\t\n185\t| Gap | Already covered? | Candidate A (default) | Candidate B | Notes |\n186\t|-----|------------------|-----------------------|-------------|-------|\n187\t| **Scroll-driven animation** | **Yes, by `motion`** (`useScroll`, `useTransform`, `scroll()`, motion-primitives ScrollProgress/InView) | motion `useScroll` (+0 kB) | GSAP ScrollTrigger (+44 kB gz measured). GSAP is now fully free incl. plugins per gsap.com. | Only choose GSAP for pinned/scrubbed multi-step timelines. Not needed for six sections. |\n188\t| **Smooth scroll** | No | **None (native scroll)** | Lenis 1.3.26 (+5.6 kB measured). `lenis/react` provides `ReactLenis`. | Lenis smoothing is **off on touch by default** (`syncTouch: false`), honors reduced motion by default (`respectReducedMotion: true`), but needs `anchors: true` for in-page nav and community reports it can feel unlike native scroll and conflict with focus/keyboard behavior. Smooth scroll is a taste item, not a requirement. Lazy-load. |\n196\t| **Lottie/Rive illustrations** | No | None | `@rive-app/react-canvas` 4.36 (interactive, state machines) | Only if designed assets exist. |\n197\t| **Analytics, forms, contact** | No | `mailto:` / static links | Formspree-style form post | No backend (out of scope). |\n198\t| **Testing** | Not requested | Vitest + Playwright (visual/mobile viewport + axe) | none | Out of the four-tool scope; suggest a mobile-viewport Lighthouse CI check in the deploy workflow. |\n199\t\n200\t## Installation\n201\t\n202\t# Scaffold (Node >=22.12 recommended)\n203\t\n204\t# Core\n205\t\n206\t# Dev\n207\t\n208\t# Then add motion-primitives components one at a time (patch type imports, move to src/)\n209\t\n210\t## Decisions needed from user\n211\t\n212\t## Risks to Carry Into Roadmap\n213\t\n214\t| Risk | Severity | Mitigation |\n215\t|------|----------|------------|\n216\t| motion 14.0.0 is 6 days old and motion releases very frequently (multiple minor versions per month) | Medium | Pin `~14.0.0`, commit lockfile, keep 12.43 / 13.5.1 as fallback versions, avoid `AnimateView` and other brand-new APIs. |\n217\t| motion-primitives is \"beta\", code is copy-in | Low | Treat as owned code. It serves recruiters/hiring managers and the design-engineering community equally: personality on the surface, key information (experience, projects, contact) reachable in seconds.\n\n**Core Value:** The site must feel crafted and memorable (tactile, physics-based motion and strong visual identity) while key info stays instantly accessible, on mobile as much as desktop.\n\n### Constraints\n\n- **Tech stack**: Vite + React + TypeScript + Tailwind — required by motion-primitives and react-spring\n- **Hosting**: GitHub Pages — static output only, needs Actions workflow or a built-output branch\n- **Platform**: Mobile must be excellent — touch interactions, performance on phones\n- **Accessibility/perf**: Assumed baseline: `prefers-reduced-motion` respected, good Lighthouse scores (to confirm with user)\n- **Process**: Detailed documentation before coding; research and surface findings for tooling decisions\n\n<!-- GSD:project-end -->\n\n<!-- GSD:stack-start source:research/STACK.md -->\n\n## Technology Stack\n\n## Bottom Line\n\n## Recommended Stack\n\n### Core Framework\n\n| Technology | Version (verified 2026-10-08) | Purpose | Why | Confidence |\n|------------|-------------------------------|---------|-----|------------|\n| Vite | `^8.3.4` | Build/dev server | Current `latest`. `create-vite` 9.2.1 scaffolds it. Rolldown-based, fast. Needs Node `^20.19 \\|\\| >=22.12`. | HIGH |\n| React / React DOM | `^19.3.0` | UI | Current `latest`. motion 14 and react-spring 10.1 both declare React 19 support. | HIGH |\n| TypeScript | `~6.0.3` (scaffold default) | Types | `create-vite` pins `~6.0`. **TS 7.0.2 (native compiler) also passed `tsc -b` on the probe**, so it is a safe drop-in later. No reason to be adventurous in v1. | HIGH |\n| @vitejs/plugin-react | `^6.1.2` | React transform | Required pairing for Vite 8 (peer `vite ^8`). | HIGH |\n| Tailwind CSS | `^4.3.3` | Styling | CSS-first config (`@import \"tailwindcss\"` + `@theme`). No `tailwind.config.js`. motion-primitives' own repo is on Tailwind v4. | HIGH |\n| @tailwindcss/vite | `^4.3.3` | Tailwind plugin | First-party Vite plugin, peer `vite ^5.2 \\|\\| ^6 \\|\\| ^7 \\|\\| ^8`. Use this, not PostCSS. | HIGH |\n| motion | `~14.0.0` | Declarative animation (what motion-primitives builds on) | Import from `motion/react`. React 19 compatible. Docs say `@react-spring/web` for React 19. | HIGH |\n| clsx + tailwind-merge | `^2.1.1` / `^3.7.0` | `cn()` helper | Required by every motion-primitives component (`import { cn } from '@/lib/utils'`). tailwind-merge v3 is the Tailwind-v4 line. | HIGH |\n| lucide-react | `^1.53.0` | UI icons | motion-primitives components import it. **It has no GitHub/LinkedIn brand icons** (probe: 0 matches), see Gaps. | HIGH |\n\n### Database\n\n### Infrastructure\n\n| Technology | Version | Purpose | Why | Confidence |\n|------------|---------|---------|-----|------------|\n| GitHub Pages, \"GitHub Actions\" source | n/a | Hosting | Required by constraints. Local machine is Node 26.9. | HIGH |\n\n### Fonts and Color\n\n| Technology | Version | Purpose | Why | Confidence |\n|------------|---------|---------|-----|------------|\n| @fontsource-variable/inter | `^5.3.0` | Self-hosted Inter variable font | Self-hosting removes the render-blocking Google Fonts request and works offline/on Pages with no third party. Family name is `'Inter Variable'`. Realtime Colors takes any Google Font name, and Fontsource packages every Google font the same way, so whatever you pick there installs as `@fontsource-variable/<name>` (or `@fontsource/<name>` for non-variable). | HIGH |\n| realtimecolors.com | web tool | Palette + type exploration | Design tool, not a dependency. See \"How to use the four tools\". | HIGH |\n\n### Supporting Libraries (recommended now)\n\n| Library | Version | Purpose | When to Use |\n|---------|---------|---------|-------------|\n| vite-imagetools | `^12.0.1` | Build-time resize + AVIF/WebP + srcset | Always, for the `images/` and `about-dj.jpg` assets. **Verified**: builds under Vite 8 and emits AVIF/WebP via `?w=400;800&format=avif;webp&as=picture`. Peer is `vite >=8`. Needs `sharp`. |\n| sharp | pulled in by imagetools (0.35.x) | Image engine | Transitive. Prefer 3 hand-inlined SVGs if only 3-4 are needed (zero deps). |\n\n### Development Tools\n\n| Tool | Purpose | Notes |\n|------|---------|-------|\n| oxlint (scaffold default) | Lint | `create-vite` 9.2.1 ships `oxlint` in `npm run lint`. Keep it. |\n| @types/node | `vite.config.ts` path alias | Needed for `node:path` / `import.meta.dirname`. |\n| Prettier + `prettier-plugin-tailwindcss` | Class sorting | Optional, recommended once Tailwind classes grow. |\n\n## Verified Bundle Costs (measured, gzipped JS, Vite 8 production build)\n\n| Build | JS gz | Delta vs. baseline |\n|-------|-------|--------------------|\n| Baseline: React 19.3 + Tailwind CSS (2.2 kB gz CSS) | 68.6 kB | n/a |\n| + react-spring only (`useSpring`, `useTrail`, `useSprings`) | 85.8 kB | **+17.2 kB** |\n| + motion with TextEffect, Tilt, Magnetic, `MotionConfig` | 121.5 kB | **+52.9 kB** |\n| + both | 138.6 kB | **+70.0 kB** |\n| Lenis (ReactLenis) alone | 74.2 kB | +5.6 kB |\n| OGL (minimal Renderer/Program/Mesh) | 81.4 kB | +12.8 kB |\n| GSAP core + ScrollTrigger | 112.7 kB | +44.1 kB |\n| three + @react-three/fiber (one box) | 309.5 kB | **+240.9 kB** |\n\n- Coexistence costs about +17 kB beyond motion. That is acceptable for a portfolio, but it is the marginal price of the \"both libraries\" decision, so use react-spring where it earns it.\n- Full `motion` component is the heavy part (about 53 kB with presence/spring/gesture features). motion.dev's own figures: `motion` about 34 kB, `m` + `LazyMotion` about 4.6 kB initial (+15 kB `domAnimation`, +25 kB `domMax`), `useAnimate` mini about 2.3 kB. **Optional optimization (not tested):** because motion-primitives is copy-pasted source, you could swap `motion.x` for `m.x` and wrap the app in `LazyMotion`. `AnimatePresence`/`layout`/`drag` need `domMax`, which erodes the saving. Do this only if Lighthouse demands it.\n- Code-split below-the-fold sections with `React.lazy` so the hero ships alone. Any 3D must be lazy-loaded (see Gaps).\n- Not measured: runtime frame rate on a real phone. That belongs in an early performance spike.\n\n## The Four Named Tools\n\n### 1. motion-primitives (ibelick) : USE, as a copy-in source library. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH\n\n- Only deps are `motion`, `clsx`, `tailwind-merge`, `lucide-react`, plus a `@/lib/utils` `cn()` helper. **No shadcn init required.**\n- Components are served as shadcn-registry JSON at `https://motion-primitives.com/c/<name>.json` (HTTP 200 verified).\n- The official CLI is `npx motion-primitives@latest add <name>`. It works (tested) but the npm package is stale (0.1.0, March 2025) and **drops files into `./components/motion-primitives/` at the project root, not `src/`**. Either move them to `src/components/motion-primitives/` or skip the CLI and copy the registry JSON content. Decide once and document it.\n- Needs the `@` alias in both `vite.config.ts` (`resolve.alias`) and `tsconfig.app.json` (`paths: { \"@/*\": [\"./src/*\"] }`). With TS 6/7, `paths` works **without** `baseUrl` (verified in the probe). shadcn's Vite doc still says to add `baseUrl`. You do not need it.\n\n### 2. react-spring : USE, scoped. Confidence: HIGH\n\n- Install `@react-spring/web@~10.1.2`. Import `useSpring, useSprings, useTrail, useChain, useTransition, animated, config` from `@react-spring/web`.\n- Version note: `react-spring` (the umbrella, 10.0.4) is behind and v11.0.0-beta (beta.1 released 2026-10-01) is **removing it**, going ESM-only, and deprecating `useTransition` in favor of new `usePresence`/`usePresenceList`. Stay on 10.1.x stable. Avoid `useTransition` for new core UI (use motion's `AnimatePresence`) so the v11 migration stays trivial.\n- Reduced motion is built in: `useReducedMotion` is exported from `@react-spring/web` (confirmed in the type definitions), and the global flag `Globals.assign({ skipAnimation: true })` snaps every spring to its target. Wire it once (snippet below).\n- Dependencies are tiny and tree-shakable. Measured at about +17 kB gz for `useSpring/useTrail/useSprings`.\n\n### 3. realtimecolors.com : USE as a design tool, with caveats. Confidence: HIGH\n\n- The seed (`050315 / fbfbfe / 2f27ce / dedcff / 433bff`, Inter/Inter) is Text, Background, Primary, Secondary, Accent. Note `050315` on `fbfbfe` is the **light** theme pairing (dark text on light bg). The tool's light/dark toggle (Alt+T) lets you design both modes in one session. The project requires light and dark, so design both explicitly rather than inverting.\n- It shows AA/AAA contrast traffic lights per role. Use them as the accessibility gate for text and interactive colors.\n- Exports: CSS variables, Tailwind CSS, SCSS, and custom templates, in HEX/RGB/HSL/OKLAB/OKLCH. The tool lists a `tailwind.config.js` style Tailwind export and does not mention v4. **Export \"CSS\" with OKLCH and paste into `@theme`**, not the Tailwind-config export:\n- Its type-scale presets (1.067 to 1.618) map to a `--text-*` ladder in `@theme`.\n- The user wants alternatives explored: do that in the design phase (Realtime Colors' scheme generators: analogous/complementary/triadic, plus randomize with Space). Keep exactly 5 role tokens plus derived tints so swapping palettes later is a single-block edit.\n\n### 4. godly.design/posts : USE as inspiration only, not a dependency. Confidence: MEDIUM\n\n- `godly.design/posts` is a curated **feed of linked social posts** (mostly X links to designers' UI interaction clips), with category nav (Websites, Tools, Templates, etc.). No dates, tags, RSS, or API were visible. Treat it as a mood-board source: capture screenshots/links into the planning docs, then extract motion patterns (spring feel, press states, cursor effects) into a written reference list before coding. (Probe also noted `godly.website` currently 301-redirects to `recent.design`, so the brand's structure may have shifted. Rely on `godly.design/posts/` and verify when you actually browse.)\n- No code or package implications.\n\n## Division of Responsibility (motion vs react-spring)\n\n| Concern | Owner | Why |\n|---------|-------|-----|\n| Mount/unmount, `AnimatePresence`, exit animations | **motion** | Mature, handles React 19 strict mode and transitions (13.1.1 and 13.4.6 fixes). |\n| Layout and shared-element transitions (`layout`, `layoutId`, Morphing Dialog) | **motion** | react-spring has no equivalent. |\n| Scroll-linked values (`useScroll`, `useTransform`, ScrollProgress, in-view reveals) | **motion** | Already bundled. 13.4.x moved offsets onto hardware-accelerated `ViewTimeline` where supported. |\n| Text/number effects, stagger reveals, all motion-primitives prefabs | **motion** | They are written against it. |\n| Simple drag, `whileTap`, `whileHover` | **motion** | Built in. No extra gesture lib. |\n| Velocity-carrying physics: flick/toss, drag-release with momentum, rubber-band, pointer-chasing follower, squish-on-press toys | **react-spring** | `useSpring` with continuous retargeting is its sweet spot and gives the \"tactile\" signature. |\n| Staggered chains of springs (`useTrail`, `useChain`) for playful sets of elements | **react-spring** | Concise API. |\n| Anything in `@react-spring/three` | **react-spring** | Only if 3D is adopted. |\n\n## Tailwind v4 Notes\n\n- v4 `hover:` only applies under `@media (hover: hover)`, so hover styles never stick after a tap on phones. This matches the \"no desktop-only gestures\" requirement. Do **not** restore v3 behavior with `@custom-variant hover (&:hover)`.\n- Browser floor: Safari 16.4+, Chrome 111+, Firefox 128+ (uses `@property`, `color-mix()`). Fine for this audience.\n- No `tailwind.config.js`; `tw-animate-css` (the shadcn replacement for tailwindcss-animate) is not needed since motion handles animation.\n\n## Dark Mode (covered: Tailwind v4 + tiny script)\n\n## Font Loading\n\n- `import '@fontsource-variable/inter/wght.css'` in `main.tsx`. Verified: it declares `font-display: swap`, `font-weight: 100 900`, and per-script `unicode-range`, so a browser downloads only the **latin subset (48 kB woff2)** for English copy (latin-ext is 85 kB and only fetched if used). Vite emits all subsets to `dist/` (harmless unused files).\n- `@fontsource-variable/inter/opsz.css` adds optical sizing (latin 73 kB). Worth it only if you want display-size tuning.\n- **Preload is not automatic**: a bare `<link rel=\"preload\" href=\"@fontsource-variable/inter/files/...\">` in `index.html` is *not* rewritten by Vite (verified, stays unresolved). If you want a preload, use a small `transformIndexHtml` plugin or `import url from '...woff2?url'`. Skipping preload is acceptable with `font-display: swap`; revisit if Lighthouse flags it.\n- If the design adds a display face from Realtime Colors, use the same Fontsource route (one variable family, latin only), and keep to 2 families total for mobile performance.\n\n## GitHub Pages Deploy\n\n## Alternatives Considered\n\n| Category | Recommended | Alternative | Why Not |\n|----------|-------------|-------------|---------|\n| Framework | Vite + React | Next.js / Astro | Next explicitly out of scope. Astro would be lighter on JS but the user wants React islands everywhere and these two React animation libs. |\n| Animation split | motion for UI choreography + react-spring for signature physics | react-spring only | Loses `layoutId`/`AnimatePresence`/scroll and every motion-primitives component. |\n| Animation split | (same) | motion only | Viable and smaller (saves 17 kB), but the user explicitly wants to try react-spring. Revisit if the spike shows react-spring adds nothing. |\n| react-spring package | `@react-spring/web` | `react-spring` umbrella | Lagging, `deprecated` tag present, removed in v11 beta. |\n| Installing primitives | Copy via registry JSON / CLI, then patch | Wait for an npm package | None exists. Source ownership is the model. |\n| Styling | Tailwind v4 | Tailwind v3.4 | Only for pre-2023 browsers. motion-primitives and shadcn are on v4. |\n| TypeScript | 6.0.x | 7.0.x | 7.0.2 compiled the probe cleanly, so it is available, but v6 is the scaffold default. Take 7 post-launch. |\n| Dark mode | Tailwind data-attr + inline script | next-themes | Next-oriented abstraction we do not need. |\n| Fonts | Fontsource self-hosted | Google Fonts `<link>` | Third-party request, render-blocking, privacy. |\n\n## What NOT to Use\n\n| Do not use | Why |\n|------------|-----|\n| `react-spring` umbrella package | Deprecated tag, removed in v11. Use `@react-spring/web`. |\n| `react-spring` v11 beta | Beta, breaking `onRest`/`immediate` semantics. Wait for stable. |\n| Raw `framer-motion` imports | motion 14 depends on `framer-motion` internally. Always import from `motion/react` so there is one copy. |\n| motion-primitives `Cursor`, `Magnetic`, `Tilt`, `Spotlight` as *primary* interactions | Mouse-only. They are desktop garnish. |\n| `shadcn init` | Unneeded; adds Radix/CVA you will not use. |\n| `tailwind.config.js`, `@tailwind base/components/utilities` | v3 patterns. |\n| `@use-gesture/react` by default | Last published March 2024, and motion already provides drag/tap/hover. Only add if a react-spring-driven drag cannot be done well otherwise. |\n| `vite-plugin-image-optimizer` as the primary image tool | Compress-only (no srcset/format variants). Last publish Oct 2025. |\n| Hard-coded absolute asset paths | Break when `base` changes. |\n| Both libraries animating the same element/property | The one real coexistence hazard. |\n\n## Gaps: capabilities the four tools do NOT cover\n\n| Gap | Already covered? | Candidate A (default) | Candidate B | Notes |\n|-----|------------------|-----------------------|-------------|-------|\n| **Scroll-driven animation** | **Yes, by `motion`** (`useScroll`, `useTransform`, `scroll()`, motion-primitives ScrollProgress/InView) | motion `useScroll` (+0 kB) | GSAP ScrollTrigger (+44 kB gz measured). GSAP is now fully free incl. plugins per gsap.com. | Only choose GSAP for pinned/scrubbed multi-step timelines. Not needed for six sections. |\n| **Smooth scroll** | No | **None (native scroll)** | Lenis 1.3.26 (+5.6 kB measured). `lenis/react` provides `ReactLenis`. | Lenis smoothing is **off on touch by default** (`syncTouch: false`), honors reduced motion by default (`respectReducedMotion: true`), but needs `anchors: true` for in-page nav and community reports it can feel unlike native scroll and conflict with focus/keyboard behavior. Smooth scroll is a taste item, not a requirement. Default: skip until the design wants it. |\n| **3D / WebGL** | No | **None** (use CSS 3D via Tilt and react-spring transforms for tactility) | (a) OGL 1.0.11 for one hero shader/blob (+12.8 kB measured); (b) three 0.186 + @react-three/fiber 9.8.1 (+241 kB gz measured, peer `react >=19 <19.4`) with `@react-spring/three` | R3F is the one thing in this list that can blow the mobile budget. If 3D is wanted, lazy-load it, cap DPR, and pause off-screen. |\n| **Gesture / drag** | **Yes, by `motion`** (`drag`, `dragConstraints`, `whileTap`, `Reorder`) | motion `drag` | `@use-gesture/react` 10.3.1 only for react-spring-driven drag | `@use-gesture` is stale (Mar 2024). react-spring has no pointer handling itself, so a react-spring drag needs it or hand-written pointer events. |\n| **Page transitions** | **Partly** (`AnimatePresence`; new `AnimateView` in motion 13.4.0 for React 19.3 `ViewTransition`, 2026-09-14, so very new) | **Single page, no router.** Use MorphingDialog/MorphingPopover for project detail | If real routes are wanted: `react-router` (library mode) or `wouter` 3.13 with hash routing + motion `AnimatePresence` | GitHub Pages cannot rewrite SPA URLs. Real routes need a `404.html` copy of `index.html` or hash routing. Note `react-router` 8.4.0 requires Node `>=22.22` and React `>=19.2.7`. `react-router-dom` 7.18.4 still publishes. `AnimateView` is LOW confidence until proven. |\n| **Image optimization** | No | **vite-imagetools 12.0.1** (verified on Vite 8) | Manual pre-resize with `sharp` script, commit outputs | Use `<picture>` with AVIF/WebP + explicit `width`/`height` + `loading=\"lazy\"` below the fold + `fetchpriority=\"high\"` on the hero/LCP image to prevent layout shift. |\n| **Theming / dark mode** | Tailwind v4 | Tailwind `@custom-variant` + inline script (above) | `next-themes` | Covered, see Dark Mode. |\n| **Icons (brand)** | lucide has none | 3-4 inline SVGs for GitHub/LinkedIn/email | `simple-icons` 16.x or `react-icons/si` 5.7 | Lucide for UI glyphs only. |\n| **Physics \"toys\" (stack/pile of objects, rigid-body)** | No | None (react-spring + motion drag can fake most) | matter-js 0.20.0 (last release 2023) or Rapier via `@react-three/rapier` | Only if a falling-objects/gravity playground is part of the identity. Lazy-load. |\n| **Lottie/Rive illustrations** | No | None | `@rive-app/react-canvas` 4.36 (interactive, state machines) | Only if designed assets exist. |\n| **Analytics, forms, contact** | No | `mailto:` / static links | Formspree-style form post | No backend (out of scope). |\n| **Testing** | Not requested | Vitest + Playwright (visual/mobile viewport + axe) | none | Out of the four-tool scope; suggest a mobile-viewport Lighthouse CI check in the deploy workflow. |\n\n## Installation\n\n# Scaffold (Node >=22.12 recommended)\n\n# Core\n\n# Dev\n\n# Then add motion-primitives components one at a time (patch type imports, move to src/)\n\n## Decisions needed from user\n\n## Risks to Carry Into Roadmap\n\n| Risk | Severity | Mitigation |\n|------|----------|------------|\n| motion 14.0.0 is 6 days old and motion releases very frequently (multiple minor versions per month) | Medium | Pin `~14.0.0`, commit lockfile, keep 12.43 / 13.5.1 as fallback versions, avoid `AnimateView` and other brand-new APIs. |\n| motion-primitives is \"beta\", code is copy-in | Low | Treat as owned code. Patch and commit, do not expect upgrades. |\n| Mouse-only interactive primitives | Medium | Design touch equivalents early (interaction phase). |\n| Combined JS (about 139 kB gz in probe, before real content and fonts) | Medium | Code-split sections, measure on a real mid-range phone in the first phase. |\n| react-spring v11 transition | Low | Pin 10.1.x, avoid `useTransition` for core UI. |\n| `lucide-react` v1 has no brand icons | Low | Inline SVGs. |\n\n## Sources\n\n- npm registry (`npm view`), 2026-10-08: versions, peer deps, dist-tags, publish dates for motion, @react-spring/web, react-spring, vite, @vitejs/plugin-react, tailwindcss, @tailwindcss/vite, typescript, lenis, gsap, three, @react-three/fiber, vite-imagetools, react-router, ogl. Decisions with a longer rationale live in the [ADR log](adr/README.md).\n6\t\n7\t## Overview\n8\t\n9\tThe site is a static, zero-backend, single-page React app served from GitHub Pages. Architecture\n\nStatus: Accepted\n\nThis document describes how the portfolio site is structured: layers, import rules, folder layout, data flow, the content model, the design-token pipeline and the animation layer. It is written so the scaffold can be built directly from it. Decisions with a longer rationale live in the [ADR log](adr/README.md).\n\n## Overview\n\nThe site is a static, zero-backend, single-page React app served from GitHub Pages. There is no router, no global store and no data fetching. The architecture comes down to four seams:\n\n1. Typed content: all copy lives in TypeScript modules checked against one schema.\n2. CSS-variable design tokens: five role colors per theme drive every surface.\n3. An internal animation layer: the only place the two animation libraries are imported.\n4. A build and deploy pipeline: Vite build, GitHub Actions, GitHub Pages.\n\nEverything else is conventional React composition with props flowing down.\n\n## Layers\n\nDependencies point down only. A layer may import from layers below it, never above.\n\n```text\n+----------------------------------------------------------------------+\n| app/        main.tsx, App.tsx, Providers, Page.tsx                    |\n|             Page.tsx is the ONLY file that imports content data       |\n+----------------------------------------------------------------------+\n| sections/   Hero, About, Experience, Projects, Skills, Education,     |\n|             Contact                      (props in, JSX out)          |\n+----------------------------------------------------------------------+\n| layout/     Container, Section, SiteHeader, PillNav, Footer,          |\n|             SkipLink, Wordmark                                        |\n+----------------------------------------------------------------------+\n| components/ ui/ (CtaLink, Toast, ThemeToggle, icons)                  |\n|             motion-primitives/ (vendored source, treated as owned)    |\n+----------------------------------------------------------------------+\n| anim/       the internal animation layer: the only place that         |\n|             imports motion/react and @react-spring/web               |\n+-------------------------------+--------------------------------------+\n| hooks/  lib/                  | styles/ (tokens.css, base.css,       |\n| (useActiveSection, useTheme,  |  index.css = tailwind + theme map)   |\n|  cn, a11y)                    |                                      |\n+-------------------------------+--------------------------------------+\n| content/ (typed TS data)      assets/ (images, fonts via Fontsource) |\n+----------------------------------------------------------------------+\nBuild and deploy: vite.config.ts, lint boundary rules, GitHub Actions\n```\n\n## Import rules\n\nOnly src/anim/ and src/components/motion-primitives/ may import motion/react or @react-spring/web.\n\nOnly src/app/Page.tsx imports content data.\n\n| Importer | May import | May not import |\n|----------|-----------|----------------|\n| sections/* | layout, components/ui, the anim barrel (src/anim/index.ts), content types (not data) | motion/react, @react-spring/*, components/motion-primitives/* directly, content data |\n| layout/*, components/ui/* | the anim barrel, lib, hooks | sections, content data |\n| components/motion-primitives/* | motion/react, lib/cn | everything else (vendor code, kept out of lint autofix churn) |\n| anim/* | motion/react, @react-spring/web, components/motion-primitives/*, the token mirror in styles | sections, layout |\n| app/Page.tsx | content data, sections | any animation library or anim internals |\n\nThese rules are enforced by oxlint's `no-restricted-imports` rule with per-folder overrides in `.oxlintrc.json`, covering dynamic `import()` and re-exports as well, so a violation fails CI rather than relying on review. Unit tests under `tests/unit` may import the libraries for assertions. A gesture library is not adopted up front. It would be added to the anim row only if a react-spring drag cannot be built well otherwise, after a React 19 compatibility check.\n\n## Folder structure\n\n```text\nrepo root\n|-- public/                  URL-stable files only: favicon set, og image,\n|                            resume.pdf (a phone-number-free export), robots.txt\n|-- index.html               meta and social tags, inline theme-init script,\n|                            noscript fallback content\n|-- 404.html                 second HTML entry: the not-found page that\n|                            GitHub Pages serves for unknown paths\n|-- vite.config.ts           base from env, Tailwind plugin, imagetools, \"@\" alias\n|-- .oxlintrc.json           import-boundary rules (see Import rules)\n|-- .github/workflows/       ci.yml, deploy.yml\n`-- src/\n    |-- main.tsx\n    |-- app/                 App.tsx, Page.tsx, Providers.tsx\n    |-- sections/            one folder per section (Hero, About, Experience,\n    |                        Projects, Skills, Education, Contact)\n    |-- layout/              Container, Section, SiteHeader, PillNav,\n    |                        Footer, SkipLink, Wordmark\n    |-- components/\n    |   |-- ui/              CtaLink, Toast, ThemeToggle, icons/ (inline SVG\n    |   |                    brand marks and UI glyphs)\n    |   `-- motion-primitives/   vendored, patched locally, logged in an ADR\n    |-- anim/\n    |   |-- index.ts         public barrel: the only animation import path\n    |   |-- MotionPreferenceProvider.tsx\n    |   |-- useMotionPreference.ts\n    |   |-- tokens.ts        springs, durations, eases in both libraries' formats\n    |   |-- MotionFeatures.tsx   LazyMotion provider with the async feature chunk\n    |   |-- declarative/     motion-owned wrappers (ToastPresence,\n    |   |                    NavIndicator, PillMorph, TextReveal)\n    |   `-- physics/         react-spring-owned wrappers (ThemeThumb and other\n    |                        pointer-driven, velocity-carrying helpers)\n    |-- content/\n    |   |-- schema.ts        every type\n    |   |-- profile.ts, experience.ts, projects.ts, skills.ts,\n    |   |   education.ts, contacts.ts     one module per section\n    |   |-- images.ts        image registry\n    |   `-- index.ts         assembles siteContent with satisfies\n    |-- hooks/               useActiveSection, useTheme\n    |-- lib/                 cn.ts, date formatting, a11y helpers\n    |-- styles/\n    |   |-- index.css        tailwind import, dark variant, theme mapping\n    |   |-- tokens.css       role tokens, derived tokens, light and dark blocks\n    |   `-- base.css         resets, focus ring, reduced-motion guard, fonts\n    `-- assets/images/       hashed by Vite\n```\n\nThe animation folder is named anim/ rather than motion/ so it is never confused with the motion package or its motion/react import path.\n\n## Data flow\n\nBuild time:\n\n```text\ncontent/*.ts --(typed, satisfies)--> app/Page --props--> sections --props--> layout,
